「想来您一定觉得难办：面对这些新发现，您怎么也想不通，简大妈手里怎么会恰好有那个小贩货囊里的那几件小玩物；也想不通那枚戒指——您很自然地以为必是奇滕登先生亲手托付给那只鸽子的——怎么会在奇滕登先生失踪的那天，鸽子飞回家时恰好系在它颈上。夫人，我们认为，必定是简大妈趁那货囊还在她棚屋后头的林子里、尚未被发现之前，先自己动手从囊中取了几样。至于另一桩，我们的解释是这样：

===Original===
“One day a young man, equipped for travelling, paused for a glass of water at the famous well in Mr. Trohm’s garden just as Mother Jane’s pigeons were picking up the corn scattered for them by the former, whose tastes are not confined to the cultivation of fruits and flowers, but extend to dumb animals, to whom he is uniformly kind. The young man wore a ring, and, being nervous, was fiddling with it as he talked to the pleasant old gentleman who was lowering the bucket for him. As he fiddled with it, the earth fell from under him, and as the daylight vanished above his head, the ring flew from his up-thrown hand, and lay, the only token of his now blotted-out existence, upon the emerald sward he had but a moment before pressed with his unsuspicious feet. It burned—this ruby burned like a drop of blood in the grass, when that demon came again to his senses, and being a telltale evidence of crime in the eyes of one who had allowed nothing to ever speak against him in these matters, he stared at it as at a deadly thing directed against himself and to be got rid of at once and by means which by no possibility could recoil back upon himself as its author.

“The pigeons stalking near offered to his abnormally acute understanding the only solution which would leave him absolutely devoid of fear. He might have swung open the lid of the well once more and flung it after its owner, but this meant an aftermath of experience from which he shrank, his delight being in the thought that the victims he saw vanish before his eyes were so many encumbrances wiped off the face of the earth by a sweep of the hand. To see or hear them again would be destructive of this notion. He preferred the subtler way and to take advantage of old Mother Jane’s characteristics, so he caught one of the pigeons (he has always been able to lure birds into his hands), and tying the ring around the neck of the bird with a blade of grass plucked up from the highway, he let it fly, and so was rid of the bauble which to Mother Jane’s eyes, of course, was a direct gift from the heavens through which the bird had flown before lighting on her doorstep.”

===Chinese===
有一天，一个一身远行装束的年轻人，在特罗姆先生园中那口出了名的井旁停下来讨一杯水喝；恰在那时，简大妈的鸽子们正啄食着谷粒——撒谷子喂它们的，正是这园子的主人。此君的雅好并不限于栽花种果，还泽及不会说话的牲灵，对它们他向来慈和。那年轻人戴着一枚戒指，心里发慌，一边同那位和气的老绅士说话——老绅士正替他把吊桶放下去——一边把戒指捻来捻去。捻弄之间，脚底下的地面塌陷了；头顶的天光一灭，戒指便从他扬起的手中飞脱，落在绿莹莹的草地上——就在片刻之前，他还用那双毫无疑心的脚踏过那片草地——成了他那转眼被抹去的存在仅存的凭证。它烧着——那颗红宝石在草丛里烧得像一滴血，就在那个恶魔回过神来的当口；而在一个从不许任何东西就这些事开口指证他的人眼里，这是一件会告发罪案的凭据；他直勾勾盯住它，仿佛盯住一件冲着他来的致命之物，必须立刻除掉，而且除掉的法子，无论如何也不能反过来落到他这个原主身上。

近旁昂首踱步的鸽子，向他那异样敏锐的心思提供了唯一一种能叫他全然无所畏惧的解决办法。他本可以再掀开井盖，把戒指扔下去还给它的主人，可那就意味着一场他畏缩不敢领教的事后经历——他的乐处，正在于默想那些当着他双眼消失的受害者，全是一挥手便从大地上拂去的累赘；再见着他们，再听到他们，都会毁了这份意趣。他宁取那条更精微的路子，利用起简大妈的脾性来。于是他捉住一只鸽子（他向来有本事把鸟儿哄进手里），用一根从大路上拔来的草，把戒指系上鸟颈，撒手放它飞去——就这样摆脱了那件小玩意儿；而这东西在简大妈眼里，自然成了一份上天直接赏下的礼物，由那鸟儿穿过长天、落到她门槛上之前一路携来。

===Original===
“Wonderful!” I exclaimed, almost overwhelmed with humiliation, but preserving a brave front. “What invention and what audacity!—the invention and the audacity of a man totally irresponsible for his deeds, was it not?” I asked. “There is no doubt, is there, about his being an absolute maniac?”

“No, madam.” What a relief I felt at that word! “Since we entrapped him yesterday and he found himself fully discovered, he has lost all grip upon himself and fills the room we put him in with the unmistakable ravings of a madman. It was through these I learned the facts I have just mentioned.”

===Chinese===
「真是匪夷所思！」我感叹道，羞惭几乎将我压倒，脸上却还端着一副镇定。「多么会编，又是何等的胆大妄为！——这编造与胆量，正出自一个对自己的行为全然不担干系的人，是不是？」我问。「他是个十足的疯子，这一点已没有疑问了吧？」

「没有疑问，夫人。」这个词给了我多大的宽慰！「自从昨天我们把他诱进罗网、他发觉自己彻底败露，他就全然把持不住自己了；我们安置他的那间屋子，被他灌满了再明白不过的疯人呓语。我方才说的那些事实，正是从这些呓语里得知的。」

===Original===
I drew a deep breath. We were standing in the sight of several men, and their presence there seemed intolerable. Unconsciously I began to walk away. Unconsciously Mr. Gryce followed me. At the end of several paces we both stopped. We were no longer visible to the crowd, and I felt I could speak the words I had been burning to say ever since I saw the true nature of Mr. Trohm’s character exposed.

“Mr. Gryce,” said I, flushing scarlet—which I here solemnly declare is something which has not happened to me before in years, and if I can help it shall never happen to me again—“I am interested in what you say, because yesterday, at his own gateway, Mr. Trohm proposed to me, and—”

===Chinese===
我深深吸了一口气。我们当时正站在几个工人望得见的地方，他们在场令我觉着难以容忍。不知不觉间，我拔步走开；不知不觉间，格赖斯先生跟了上来。走出几步远，两人都停住了。人群已望不见我们；我觉得，自从亲眼见到特罗姆先生的真面目败露以来，一直烧在我心头、急于出口的那些话，如今总算可以说了。

「格赖斯先生，」我说，脸涨得通红——我在此郑重声明，这样的事多年来不曾在我身上发生过，而且只要我做得了主，往后绝不容它再发生——「我对您方才这番话感兴趣，是因为昨天，就在他自己家那道栅门前，特罗姆先生向我求了婚，而且——」

===Original===
“You did not accept him?”

“No. What do you think I am made of, Mr. Gryce? I did not accept him, but I made the refusal a gentle one, and—this is not easy work, Mr. Gryce,” I interrupted myself to say with suitable grimness—“the same thing took place between me and Deacon Spear, and to him I gave a response such as I thought his presumption warranted. The discrimination does not argue well for my astuteness, Mr. Gryce. You see, I crave no credit that I do not deserve. Perhaps you cannot understand that, but it is a part of my nature.”

===Chinese===
「您没有应他？」

「没有。您当我是用什么做的，格赖斯先生？我没有应他，不过回绝得委婉，而且——这实在不是桩容易的差事，格赖斯先生，」我插进这么一句，面上摆出相称的冷峻——「我和斯皮尔执事之间也来过同样一出，对他，我的答复便是我认为他那放肆应得的了。这般厚此薄彼，实在显不出我的精明，格赖斯先生。您瞧，不该我得的称誉，我一概不贪。这层您或许理会不了，可这是我天性的一部分。」

===Original===
“Madam,” said he, and I must own I thought his conduct perfect, “had I not been as completely deceived as yourself I might find words of criticism for this possibly unprofessional partiality. But when an old hand like myself can listen to the insinuations of a maniac, and repose, as I must say I did repose, more or less confidence in the statements he chose to make me, and which were true enough as to the facts he mentioned, but wickedly false and preposterously wrong in suggestion, I can have no words of blame for a woman who, whatever her understanding and whatever her experience, necessarily has seen less of human nature and its incalculable surprises. As to the more delicate matter you have been good enough to confide to me, madam, I have but one remark to make. With such an example of womanhood suddenly brought to their notice in such a wild as this, how could you expect them, sane or insane, to do otherwise than they did? I know many a worthy man who would like to follow their example.” And with a bow that left me speechless, Mr. Gryce laid his hand on his heart and softly withdrew.

===Chinese===
「夫人，」他说——我必须承认，我觉得他这番应对无可挑剔——「倘若我不是和您一样彻头彻尾受了骗，这桩或许有失职业本分的偏心，我原还能找出几句批评的话来。可是，连我这样一只老手，尚且听得进一个疯子的含沙射影，而且——我必须承认，我确实——多少信了他选择说给我的那些话：那些话就他所举的事实而言倒也够真，可那弦外之音却恶毒地假、荒唐地错；既然如此，对一位女士，我还有什么责备的话说得出呢？无论她的见识如何、阅历如何，她见过的人心及其种种不可估量的意外，总归注定比我少。至于承蒙您见告的那桩更微妙的事，夫人，我只有一句话要说。在这样一片荒野之地，忽然有您这样一位女性的典范出现在他们眼前，您还能指望他们——不论神志清明还是癫狂——做出别的什么来吗？我知道多少正派男子，巴不得效法他们二位呢。」说罢，格赖斯先生一躬身，躬得我哑口无言；他一手按住心口，悄悄地退了出去。

### Epilogue / 尾声

===Original===
Some Stray Leaflets from an Old Diary of Althea Knollys, Found by Me in the Packet Left in My Charge by Her Daughter Lucetta.

===Chinese===
以下散页，摘自阿尔西娅·诺利斯的一本旧日记，是我在她女儿露塞塔留交我保管的那只纸包里发现的。

===Original===
> I never thought I should do so foolish a thing as begin a diary. When in my boarding-school days (which I am very glad to be rid of) I used to see Meeley Butterworth sit down every night of her life over a little book which she called the repository of her daily actions, I thought that if ever I reached that point of imbecility I would deserve to have fewer lovers and more sense, just as she so frequently advised me to. And yet here I am, pencil in hand, jotting down the nothings of the moment, and with every prospect of continuing to do so for two weeks at least. For (why was I born such a chatterbox!) I have seen my fate, and must talk to someone about him, if only to myself, nature never having meant me to keep silence on any living topic that interests me.
> Yes, with lovers in Boston, lovers in New York, and a most determined suitor on the other side of our own home-walls in Peekskill, I have fallen victim to the grave face and methodical ways of a person I need not name, since he is the only gentleman in this whole town, except—But I won’t except anybody. Charles Knollys has no peer here or anywhere, and this I am ready to declare, after only one sight of his face and one look from his eye, though to no one but you, my secret, noncommittal confidant—for to acknowledge to any human being that my admiration could be caught, or my heart touched, by a person who had not sued two years at my feet, would be to abdicate an ascendency I am so accustomed to I could not see it vanish without pain. Besides, who knows how I shall feel tomorrow? Meeley Butterworth never shows any hesitation in uttering her opinion either of men or things, but then her opinion never changes, whilst mine is a very thistledown, blowing hither and thither till I cannot follow its wanderings myself. It is one of my charms, certain fools say, but that is nonsense. If my cheeks lacked color and my eyes were without sparkle, or even if I were two inches taller instead of being the tiniest bit of mortal flesh to be found amongst all the young ladies of my age in our so-called society, I doubt if the lightness of my mind would meet with the approbation of even the warmest woman-lovers of this time. As it is, it just passes, and sometimes, as tonight, for instance, when I can hardly see to inscribe these lines on this page for the vision of two grave, if not quietly reproving eyes which float between it and me, I almost wish I had some of Meeley’s responsible characteristics, instead of being the airiest, merriest, and most volatile being that ever tried to laugh down the grandeur of this dreary old house with its century of memories.
> Ah! that allusion has given me something to say. This house. What is there about it except its size to make a stranger like me look back continually over her shoulder in going down the long halls, or even when nestling comfortably by the great wood-fire in the immense drawing-room? *I* am not one of your fanciful ones; but I can no more help doing this, than I can help wishing Judge Knollys lived in a less roomy mansion with fewer echoing corners in its innumerable passages. *I* like brightness and cheer, at least in my surroundings. If I must have gloom, or a seriousness which some would call gloom, let me have it in individuals where there is some prospect of a blithe, careless-hearted little midget effecting a change, and not in great towering walls and endless floors which no amount of sunshine or laughter could ever render homelike, or even comfortable.
> But there! If one has the man, one must have the home, so I had better say no more against the home till I am quite sure I do not want the man. For—Well, well, I am not a fool, but I *did* hear something just then, a something which makes me tremble yet, though I have spent five good minutes trilling the gayest songs I know.
> I think it is very inconsiderate of the witches to bother thus a harmless mite like myself, who only asks for love, light, and money enough to buy a ribbon or a jewel when the fancy takes her, which is not as often as my enemies declare. And now a question! Why are my enemies always to be found among the girls, and among the plainest of them too? I never heard a man say anything against me, though I have sometimes surprised a look on their faces (I saw it today) which might signify reproof if it were not accompanied by a smile showing anything but displeasure.

===Chinese===
> 我从没想过自己会干出记日记这种傻事。当年在寄宿学校念书的时候（那段日子总算摆脱了，真叫我高兴），我眼见米莉·巴特沃斯每天夜里雷打不动地坐到一本小簿子跟前——她管那本子叫她每日言行的宝库——我就想，倘若我有一天也蠢到这个地步，那便活该情人少些、脑子多些，正像她时常劝我的那样。可如今我却坐在这儿，手捏铅笔，记下眼前这些无聊琐事，而且眼看至少还要这样记上两个星期。因为（谁叫我天生这么个碎嘴子！）我已见到了我的命运，总得找人说说他，哪怕只说给自己听——造物主从来就没打算叫我在任何使我感兴趣的活题目上守口如瓶。
> 不错，波士顿有我的情人，纽约有我的情人，皮克斯基尔我们家墙那头还有一位百折不回的追求者，我却拜倒在一张严肃的面孔、一套一丝不苟的做派跟前；那人是谁，我无须点名，因为他是全城独一份的绅士，除了——不，我谁也不除外。查尔斯·诺利斯在此地也好，在天底下也罢，都无可匹敌；这一点我随时乐于宣告——尽管只见过他一面、只接过他一个眼色——可这话只说给你听，我的隐秘而不置可否的知己；因为若向任何一个活人承认，我的倾慕竟会被一个不曾在我脚下求告两年的人捕获，我的心竟会被他打动，那就等于放弃一种我太习惯了的优越地位，眼看它消逝，我不可能不感到痛苦。何况，谁知道我明天又是什么心情？米莉·巴特沃斯无论议论人还是议论事，吐露意见从不迟疑，可她的意见也从不变更；我的意见却活脱脱一团蓟花冠毛，飘来荡去，连我自己也追不上它的行踪。有些蠢货说，这是我的迷人处之一，纯属胡说。倘若我双颊缺少血色，眼睛没有光彩，或者哪怕我再高上两英寸，而不是在同龄的所谓上流社会年轻小姐中间数得着的顶顶小巧的一段血肉之躯，我很怀疑我这颗轻飘飘的头脑，能否博得时下最爱慕女性的男人们的赞许。如今这样，也仅仅勉强及格。可有的时候，比如今夜，两只严肃的、若非悄然责备的眼睛浮在这页纸与我之间，弄得我几乎看不清落笔的字迹，我几乎巴不得自己也有一点米莉那种可靠持重的品性，而不是像眼下这样，是天下最轻飘、最快活、最没定性的活物，妄想用笑声压倒这座阴森老宅的气派——连同它一个世纪的种种记忆。
> 啊！这一提倒给了我话头。这座宅子。除了它的大，究竟还有什么，能叫我这样一个陌生人在走过长长的过道时不住地回头张望，甚至在大客厅熊熊的柴火边舒舒服服偎着的时候也这样？*我*可不是你们那类想入非非的人；可我忍不住要这样做，正如我忍不住盼望诺利斯法官（Judge Knollys）住的是一座不那么宽敞的公馆，里面数不清的走廊间少几个回声嗡嗡的角落。*我*喜欢亮堂和欢快，至少在我周遭是这样。倘若我注定要与阴郁为伍，或者与某种别人会称作阴郁的严肃为伍，那就让阴郁长在人身上好了——长在人身上，一个快活而没心没肺的小不点儿尚有几分指望使它改观；而不要长在高耸入云的四壁和望不到头的层层楼面里——多少阳光、多少笑声，也休想把那样的地方变成家的模样，甚至连舒服二字也谈不上。
> 不过算了！既然要这个人，就得要这个家；所以我最好别再数落这个家，等我十分确定自己不要这个人的时候再数落不迟。因为——唉，唉，我不是傻子，可我方才*确实*听见了什么，那什么到现在还叫我发抖，尽管我足足花了五分钟，哼着我所知道的最欢快的歌儿。
> 我想，那些巫婆真是欠考虑，竟这样来纠缠我这般无害的小东西：我只求有爱情，有光亮，有兴头来了够买一条缎带或一件小首饰的钱——而那兴头，可远不像我的冤家们宣称的那样来得勤。现在问一个问题！为什么我的冤家总是出在姑娘们中间，而且偏偏是其中最不标致的那些？我从没听哪个男人说过我一句坏话，虽然我有时冷不防撞见他们脸上的一种神情（今天我就撞见了），那神情若不是伴着一丝绝非不快的微笑，简直要算是一种责备了。

===Original===
> But this is a digression, as Meeley would say. What I want to do, but which I seem to find it very difficult to do, is to tell how I came to be here, and what I have seen since I came. First, then, to be very short about the matter, I am here because the old folks—that is, my father and Mr. Knollys, have decided Charles and I should know each other. In thought, I courtesy to the decision; I think we ought to too. For while many other men are handsomer or better known, or have more money, alas! than he, he alone has a way of drawing up to one’s side with an air that captivates the eye and sets the heart trembling, a heart, moreover, that never knew before it could tremble, except in the presence of great worldly prosperity and beautiful, beautiful things. So, as this experience is new, I am dutifully obliged for the excitement it gives me, and am glad to be here, awesome as the place is, and destitute of any such pleasures as I have been accustomed to in the gay cities where I have hitherto spent most of my time.
> But there! I am rambling again. I have come to X, as you now see, for good and sufficient reasons, and while this house is one of consequence and has been the resort of many notable people, it is a little lonesome, our only neighbor being a young man who has a fine enough appearance, but who has already shown his admiration of me so plainly—of course he was in the road when I drove up to the house—that I lost all interest in him at once, such a nonsensical liking at first sight being, as I take it, a tribute only to my audacious little travelling bonnet and the curl or two which will fall out on my cheek when I move my head about too quickly, as I certainly could not be blamed for doing, in driving into a place where I was expected to make myself happy for two weeks.
> He, then, is out of these chronicles. When I say his name is Obadiah Trohm, you will probably be duly thankful. But he is not as stiff and biblical as his name would lead you to expect. On the contrary, he is lithe, graceful, and suave to a point which makes Charles Knollys’ judicial face a positive relief to the eye and such little understanding as has been accorded me.
> I cannot write another word. It is twelve o’clock, and though I have the cosiest room in the house, all chintz and decorated china, I find myself listening and peering just as I did downstairs in their great barn of a drawing-room. I wonder if any very dreadful things ever happened in this house? I will ask old Mr. Knollys tomorrow, or—or Mr. Charles.

===Chinese===
> 不过这是题外话，米莉会这么说的。我想做、却又似乎很难做到的，是讲清我怎么会到这里来，来了以后又见到了些什么。那么，长话短说：我之所以在这里，是因为两家老人——也就是我的父亲和诺利斯老先生——决定让查尔斯和我彼此认识认识。我在心里向这个决定行了个屈膝礼；我想我们也理当如此。因为，唉，虽说别的许多男人比他更俊美，或更有名望，或更有钱，唯独他有那么一副做派：悄然凑到人身旁，那份气韵摄人眼目、撼人心颤——何况这颗心，从前从不晓得自己还会发颤，除非是站在泼天的富贵和美之又美的东西跟前。既然这番体验是新鲜的，它带给我的激动，我便恭恭敬敬地领受了；我也乐意待在这里，哪怕这地方令人生畏，又全然没有我此前消磨了大半光阴的那些花花世界里惯享的乐趣。
> 不过得了！我又扯远了。你如今看得明白，我来到 X 镇，理由充分而正当。这座宅子是有身份的宅第，多少显赫人物曾是它的座上客，却未免有点冷清：我们唯一的邻居是个年轻人，仪表倒也够堂堂，可他已把自己的倾慕表露得太露骨——我驱车到宅门前时，他偏偏就站在路旁——顿时叫我对他兴味索然。依我看，这种一见倾心的胡闹，无非是向我那顶大胆的小旅行软帽、外加那一两绺鬈发敬献的殷勤——我一转头转得太急，它们就从鬓边滑到腮上来；而驱车进入一个指望我快活上两个星期的地方时，脑袋转得急些，总归怪不得我吧。
> 那么，此人就从这部编年史里除名吧。等我说出他的名字是奥巴代亚·特罗姆，你多半会如释重负地承我的情。不过他并不像他的名字让人料想的那样刻板、那样满身《圣经》气。恰恰相反，他身段轻灵，风度翩翩，温文得过了头，倒叫查尔斯·诺利斯那张法官式的面孔，无论对我的眼睛、还是对我天生分得的那一点点悟性，都成了一种实实在在的宽慰。
> 再写不动一个字了。已经十二点了。虽说我在宅子里住的是顶适意的一间屋，满屋印花布陈设和彩绘瓷器，我却发现自己又在侧耳细听、睁眼窥望，跟先前在楼下他们那谷仓似的大客厅里一模一样。不知道这宅子里可曾出过什么了不得的可怕事情？明天我要问问诺利斯老先生，或者——或者问问查尔斯先生。

===Original===
> I am sorry I was so inquisitive; for the stories Charles told me—I thought I had better not trouble the old gentleman—have only served to people the shadows of this rambling old house with figures of whose acquaintance I am likely to be more or less shy. One tale in particular gave me the shivers. It was about a mother and daughter who both loved the same man (it seems incredible, girls so seldom seeing with the eyes of their mothers), and it was the daughter who married him, while the mother, brokenhearted, fled from the wedding and was driven up to the great door, here, in a coach, dead. They say that the coach still travels the road just before some calamity to the family—a phantom coach which floats along in shadow, turning the air about it to mist that chills the marrow in the bones of the unfortunate who sees it. I am going to see it myself some day, the real coach, I mean, in which this tragic event took place. It is still in the stable, Charles tells me. I wonder if I will have the courage to sit where that poor devoted mother breathed out her miserable existence. I shall endeavor to do so if only to defy the fate which seems to be closing in upon me.
> Charles is an able lawyer, but his argument in favor of close bonnets versus bewitching little pokes with a rose or two in front, was very weak, I thought, today. He seemed to think so himself, after a while; for when, as the only means of convincing him of the weakness of the cause he was advocating, I ran upstairs and put on a poke similar to the aforesaid, he retracted at once and let the case go by default. For which I, and the poke, made suitable acknowledgments, to the great amusement of papa Knollys, who was on my side from the first.
> Not much going on today. Yet I have never felt merrier. Oh, ye hideous, bare old walls! Won’t I make you ring if—

===Chinese===
> 我后悔自己那么多嘴好问；因为查尔斯讲给我的那些故事——我想还是不去搅扰老先生的好——唯一的用处，就是给这座七拐八绕的老宅的阴影里住满了人影，而这些人影，我多半是要怯于结识的。有一个故事尤其叫我起了鸡皮疙瘩。说的是母女二人爱上了同一个男人（这似乎难以置信，姑娘们极少用她们母亲的眼睛看东西），嫁给那人的是女儿，做母亲的心碎肠断，从婚礼上逃出来，被马车载到——正是此地——大宅门前时，已是一具死尸。人们说，那辆马车如今仍在家中大祸将临之前行经那条路——一辆幽灵马车，在暗影里飘飘滑行，把周身的空气化成一团寒雾，凉透那目击它的倒霉人的骨髓。我打算哪天亲自去看看它，我是说那辆真马车，那桩惨事发生时的所在。它还在马厩里，查尔斯告诉我的。不知道我可有胆量，坐一坐那位可怜而痴心的母亲咽下她悲惨一生最后一口气的位子。我要尽力坐一坐，哪怕只为藐视一下那仿佛正朝我四面围拢来的命运。
> 查尔斯是位能干的律师，可他今天那番替严实女帽辩护、驳斥前缀一两朵玫瑰的撩人小前伸帽的论证，依我看，实在软弱得很。过了一会儿，他自己似乎也觉出来了；因为，为叫他明白他所辩护的立场何其站不住脚——这是唯一的法子——我跑上楼去，戴上一顶与上述无二的前伸帽，他当即收回成命，听凭本案缺席判负。对此，我和那顶前伸帽一道致以恰如其分的谢忱，逗得诺利斯老爹好不开心——他从头就站在我这一边。
> 今天没什么事。可我从不曾这样快活过。哦，你们这些丑陋的光秃秃的老墙壁啊！等着吧，看我叫不叫你们发出回响，只要——

===Original===
> I won’t have it! I won’t have that smooth, persistent hypocrite pushing his way into my presence, when my whole heart and attention belong to a man who would love me if he only could get his own leave to do so. Obadiah Trohm has been here today, on one pretext or another, three times. Once he came to bring some very choice apples—as if I cared for apples! The second time he had a question of great importance, no doubt, to put to Charles, and as Charles was in my company, the whole interview lasted, let us say, a good half-hour at least. The third time he came, it was to see *me*, which, as it was now evening, meant talk, talk, talk in the great drawing-room, with just a song interpolated now and then, instead of a cosy chat in the window-seat of the pretty Flower Parlor, with only one pair of ears to please and one pair of eyes to watch. Master Trohm was intrusive, and, if no one felt it but myself, it is because Charles Knollys has set himself up an ideal of womanhood to which I am a contradiction. But that will not affect the end. A woman may be such a contradiction and yet win, if her heart is in the struggle and she has, besides, a certain individuality of her own which appeals to the eye and heart if not to the understanding. I do not despair of seeing Charles Knollys’ forehead taking a very deep frown at sight of his handsome and most attentive neighbor. Heigho! why don’t I answer Meeley Butterworth’s last letter? Am I ashamed to tell her that I have to limit my effusion to just four pages because I have commenced a diary?

> I declare I begin to regard it a misfortune to have dimples. I never have regarded it so before when I have seen man after man succumb to them, but *now* they have become my bane, for they attract two admirers, just at the time they should attract but one, and it is upon the wrong man they flash the oftenest; why, I leave it to all true lovers to explain. As a consequence, Master Trohm is beginning to assume an air of superiority, and Charles, who may not believe in dimples, but who on that very account, perhaps, seems to be always on the lookout for them, shrinks more or less into the background, as is not becoming in a man with so many claims to respect, if not to love. *I* want to feel that each one of these precious fourteen days contains all that it can of delight and satisfaction, and how can I when Obadiah—oh, the charming and romantic name!—holds my crewels, instead of Charles, and whispers words which, coming from other lips, would do more than waken my dimples!
> But if I must have a suitor, just when a suitor is not wanted, let me at least make him useful. Charles shall read his own heart in this man’s passion.

===Chinese===
> 我不答应！我不许那个八面玲珑、死缠烂打的伪君子硬挤到我眼前来，如今我的整颗心、整个心思，都属于一个男人——一个只要他自己肯点头批准、就会爱我的男人。奥巴代亚·特罗姆今天到这儿来了，借着这个由头、那个由头，来了三趟。头一回是送来一些上好的苹果——好像我在乎苹果似的！第二回，他无疑有一桩顶要紧的问题要请教查尔斯，而查尔斯正陪着我，于是那一场会晤，就算它足足持续了至少半个钟头吧。第三回来，为的是看*我*；这时已是晚上，那就意味着在大客厅里谈啊、谈啊、谈个没完，中间偶尔插上一支歌，而不是在那间漂亮花房的窗座上舒舒服服地闲话，只需讨好一双耳朵、只需留神一双眼睛。特罗姆少爷实在冒失；倘若只有我一个人觉出来，那是因为查尔斯·诺利斯给自己竖起了一套理想女性的标准，而我恰恰是那标准的反面。可这妨碍不了结局。一个女人纵然是这样的反面，照样能赢，只要她的心在这场较量里，再者她自身还有几分个性，纵然说不进头脑，也进得了眼睛和心。我并不灰心，总有一天会看见查尔斯·诺利斯的额头，一见到他那位英俊而殷勤备至的邻居，便拧起一道深深的皱纹。哎呀！我怎么还不回米莉·巴特沃斯的上一封信？难道我不好意思告诉她，因为开始记日记，我的鸿篇抒情只得压缩到区区四页了么？

> 我郑重宣布，我开始把长酒窝看作一桩不幸了。从前眼看一个男人接着一个男人拜倒在它们之下，我从不这么想；可*如今*它们成了我的祸根：它们引来两个倾慕者，偏偏在只该引来一个的时候，而且它们最常在那错误的人面前闪烁；缘故何在，我留给天下真心相恋的人去解释。其结果便是，特罗姆少爷开始摆出一副居高临下的气派，而查尔斯——他兴许不信酒窝，可也许正因如此，反倒似乎时时刻刻提防着它们——却多少退到了幕后；一个如此值得敬重、若不说值得爱慕的男人，这副做派可不相称。*我*巴望着这宝贵的十四天里，每一天都盛满它盛得下的欢喜和满足；可当替我捧着绒线的不是查尔斯，而是奥巴代亚——哦，这迷人而浪漫的名字！——当他低声说出那些话——换成别人的嘴唇说出来，可就不止是唤醒我的酒窝那么简单了——我又如何能办到？
> 可倘若偏偏在用不着追求者的时候来了追求者，那至少让我把他派上些用场。查尔斯自会从这个人的痴情里，读出他自己的心。

===Original===
> I don’t know why, but I have taken a dislike to the Flower Parlor. It now vies with the great drawing-room in my disregard. Yesterday, in crossing it, I felt a chill, so sudden and so penetrating, that I irresistibly thought of the old saying, “Someone is walking over my grave.” *My grave!* where lies it, and why should I feel the shudder of it now? Am I destined to an early death? The bounding life in my veins says no. But I never again shall like that room. It has made me think.

===Chinese===
> 不知为什么，我厌恶起花房来了。如今在我心里的嫌弃上，它已足与大客厅一争高下。昨天穿堂而过时，我感到一阵寒意，来得那样突兀，那样彻骨，我不由得想起那句老话：「有人正从我的坟上走过。」*我的坟！*它在哪里？为什么如今我就该感到它的战栗？难道我命里注定早亡？我血管里欢蹦乱跳的生机说：不。可我再也不会喜欢那个房间了。它叫我起了心思。

===Original===
> I have not only sat in the old coach, but I had (let me drop the words slowly, they are so precious) I—I have had—a *kiss*—given me there. Charles gave me this kiss; he could not help it. I was sitting on the seat in front, in a sort of mock mirth he was endeavoring to frown upon, when suddenly I glanced up and our eyes met, and—He says it was the sauciness of my dimples (oh, those old dimples! they seem to have stood me in good stead after all); but I say it was my sincere affection which drew him, for he stooped like a man forgetful of everything in the whole wide world but the little trembling, panting being before him, and gave me one of those caresses which seals a woman’s fate forever, and made me, the featherbrained and thoughtless coquette, a slave to this large-minded and truehearted man for all my life hereafter.
> Why I should be so happy over this event is beyond my understanding. That he should be in the seventh heaven of delight is only to be expected, but that I should find myself tripping through this gloomy old house like one treading on air is a mystery, to the elucidation of which I can only give my dimples. My reason can make nothing out of it. I, who thought of nothing short of a grand establishment in Boston, money, servants, and a husband who would love me blindly whatever my faults, have given my troth—you will say my lips, but the one means the other—to a man who will never be known outside of his own county, never be rich, never be blind even, for he frowns upon me as often as he smiles, and, worst of all, who lives in a house so vast and so full of tragic suggestion that it might well awaken doleful anticipations in much more serious-minded persons than myself.
> And yet I am happy, so happy that I have even attempted to make the acquaintance of the grim old portraits and weak pastels which line the walls of many of these bedrooms. Old Mr. Knollys caught me courtesying just now before one of these ancestral beauties, whose face seemed to hold a faint prophecy of my own, and perceiving by my blushes that this was something more than a mere childish freak on my part, he chucked me under the chin and laughingly asked, how long it was likely to be before he might have the honor of adding my pretty face to the collection. Which should have made me indignant, only I am not in an indignant mood just now.

===Chinese===
> 我不但坐过了那辆旧马车，还得了（让我把这几个字慢慢吐出来，它们太珍贵了）我——我得着了——一个*吻*——就是在那儿给我的。这个吻是查尔斯给的；他情不自禁。我当时坐在前头的座位上，正装出一副他板着脸想呵斥的假欢欣，忽然我抬眼一望，两人的目光撞在了一起，于是——他说是我的酒窝太俏皮（哦，那两个老酒窝！看来到底没少帮我的忙）；我却说，吸住他的是我的真心，因为他俯下身来的样子，像个把天底下一切都忘干净了的男人，只记得眼前这具微微发颤、气喘吁吁的小小生命，给了我那样一种把女人命运永远封缄了的爱抚，叫我这个没头脑、不用心的卖弄风情女子，做了这个襟怀阔大、心地真诚的男人的奴隶，此后一生，再无更改。
> 这桩事何以叫我如此快活，超出我的理解。他乐上七重天，那在意料之中；可我竟发现自己像踩着云絮似的，轻盈盈小跑着穿过这座阴沉沉的老宅，这就成了一个谜——要解这个谜，我拿得出手的只有我的酒窝。我的理性在这儿一无所获。我这个人，从前念念不忘的，无非是波士顿的豪门华第、金钱、仆役，和一个无论我有多少毛病都盲目爱我的丈夫，如今却把我的信约——你会说，我的嘴唇，可其一便意味着其二——交付给了一个男人：他这辈子也走不出本县的名声，发不了财，甚至连盲目也谈不上，因为他冲我皱眉的次数同微笑一样多；最糟的是，他住在一座这样庞大、这样浸透悲剧暗示的宅子里，哪怕换成心思远比我沉重的人，也尽会在里头生出凄凉的预感来。
> 然而我快活，快活到竟然起意去结识那些阴森的老画像和苍白无力的粉彩画，它们挂满了这里许多间卧室的墙。方才诺利斯老先生撞见我正对着其中一位祖上美人行屈膝礼——那张脸似乎藏着一道关于我自己的淡淡预言——他从我的红晕里看出，这不止是我一时孩子气的胡闹，便伸指在我颔下轻轻一挑，笑着问我，还要过多久，他才有幸把我这张俏脸添进这批收藏里去。这话本该叫我恼怒，只可惜我此刻偏偏没有恼怒的心情。

===Original===
> Why have I been so foolish? Why did I not let my overfond neighbor know from the beginning that I detested him, instead of—But what have I done anyway? A smile, a nod, a laughing word mean nothing. When one has eyes which persist in dancing in spite of one’s every effort to keep them demure, men who become fools are apt to call one a coquette, when a little good sense would teach them that the woman who smiles always has some other way of showing her regard to the man she really favors. I could not help being on merry terms with Mr. Trohm, if only to hide the effect another’s presence has on me. But he thinks otherwise, and today I had ample reason for seeing why his good looks and easy manners have invariably awakened distrust in me rather than admiration. Master Trohm is vindictive, and I should be afraid of him, if I had not observed in him the presence of another passion which will soon engross all his attention and make him forget me as soon as ever I become Charles’ wife. Money is his idol, and as fortune seems to favor him, he will soon be happy in the mere pleasure of accumulation. But this is not relating what happened today.
> We were walking in the shrubbery (by *we* I naturally mean Charles and myself), and he was saying things which made me at the same time happy and a bit serious, when I suddenly felt myself under the spell of some baleful influence that filled me with a dismay I could neither understand nor escape from.
> As this could not proceed from Charles, I turned to look about me, when I encountered the eyes of Obadiah Trohm, who was leaning on the fence separating his grounds from those of Mr. Knollys, looking directly at us. If I flinched at this surveillance, it was but the natural expression of my indignation. His face wore a look calculated to frighten anyone, and though he did not respond to the gesture I made him, I felt that my only chance of escaping a scene was to induce Charles to leave me before he should see what I saw in the lowering countenance of his intrusive neighbor. As the situation demanded self-possession and the exercise of a ready wit, and as these are qualities in which I am not altogether deficient, I succeeded in carrying out my intention sooner even than I expected. Charles hurried from my presence at the first word, and proceeded towards the house without seeing Trohm, and I, quivering with dread, turned towards the man whom I felt, rather than saw, approaching me.
> He met me with a look I shall never forget. I have had lovers—too many of them—and this is not the first man I have been compelled to meet with rebuff and disdain, but never in the whole course of my none too extended existence have I been confronted by such passion or overwhelmed with such bitter recrimination. He seemed like a man beside himself, yet he was quiet, too quiet, and while his voice did not rise above a whisper, and he approached no nearer than the demands of courtesy required, he produced so terrifying an effect upon me that I longed to cry for help, and would have done so, but that my throat closed with fright, and I could only gurgle forth a remonstrance, too faint even for him to hear.

===Chinese===
> 我怎么这样傻？为什么我不从一开始就叫那位过分痴缠的邻居明白我嫌恶他，而不是——可我到底做过什么呢？一个微笑，一个点头，一句玩笑话，什么都算不上。一个人的眼睛偏要顾盼飞舞，任你怎么叫它们端庄也拗不过，变成傻瓜的男人就容易骂她卖弄风情；其实稍有一点头脑就该明白：一个总是微笑的女人，自有别的法子向她真正属意的男人表露心意。我同特罗姆先生有说有笑，实在是出于无奈，只为遮掩另一个人在场时在我身上引起的变化。可他偏不这么想；而今天，我也充分看清了缘由，为什么他那副好相貌、那套随和的做派，一向在我心里唤起的是戒心而非倾慕。特罗姆少爷睚眦必报；若不是我看出来他心里另有一宗痴情，不久便要占尽他的心思，等我一旦做了查尔斯的妻子就立刻忘了我，我本该怕他的。金钱是他的偶像；时运似乎又眷顾他，他很快就会沉醉在单纯的积攒之乐里，自得其乐。可这就不是在讲今天的事了。
> 我们正在灌木丛间散步（这个*我们*，自然是指查尔斯和我），他说着一些叫我同时又快活、又有些认真起来的话，忽然间，我感到自己落进了某种凶煞之力的魔掌，一种我既无从理解、也无从逃脱的惶恐把我灌满了。
> 这感觉既不会来自查尔斯，我便转头四顾，一头撞上了奥巴代亚·特罗姆的目光——他正倚在那道隔开他家园圃与诺利斯家场院的栅栏上，直勾勾望着我们。倘若我在这一监视下瑟缩了一下，那也不过是我义愤的自然流露。他脸上摆着一副存心吓人的神色；他对我做出的手势未加理会，可我明白，要免掉一场当场的吵闹，我唯一的机会，就是趁查尔斯还没看见我在他那唐突邻居阴沉面色里看见的东西，先劝查尔斯离开我。这局面需要沉着，也需要急智，而这两样本事我并非全然没有，于是我竟比预想更快地成了事。查尔斯听了我头一句话便匆匆去了，径直朝宅子走去，没有看见特罗姆；而我，抖着满身的恐惧，转向那个我与其说看见、不如说感觉到正朝我走来的人。
> 他迎上我来的那一眼，我终生难忘。我的情人有过——太多了——被迫以冷面和轻蔑相见的男人，这也不是头一个；可在我这段说长不长的人生里，我从不曾面对面遇上这样的激情，不曾被这样刻毒的责骂压倒过。他像个失了魂的人，却又安静，安静得过了头；他的声音始终不曾高过耳语，他的身子也始终停在礼数要求的距离之外，可他在我身上激起的恐怖如此之大，我渴望喊救命，也当真会喊的，若不是喉咙吓得发紧，只咯咯地挤出一句抗议，微弱得连他也听不见。

===Original===
> “You have played with a man’s best feelings,” he said. “You have led me to believe that I had only to speak to have you for my own. Are you simply foolish, or are you wicked? Did you care for me at all, or was it only your wish to increase the number of men in your train? This one” (here his hand pointed quiveringly towards the house) “has enjoyed a happiness denied me. His hand has touched yours, his lips—” Here his words became almost unintelligible till his purpose gave him strength, and he cried: “But notwithstanding this, notwithstanding any vows you may have exchanged, I have claims upon you that I will not yield. I who have loved no woman before you, will have such a hand in your fate that you will never be able to separate yourself from the influence I shall exert over you. I will not intrude between you and your lover; I will not affect dislike or disturb your outer life with any vain display of my hatred or my passion, but I will work upon your secret thoughts, and create a slowly increasing dread in the inner sanctuary of your heart till you wish you had called up the deadliest of serpents in your pathway rather than the latent fury of Obadiah Trohm. You are a girl now; when you are married and become a mother, you will understand me. For the present I leave you. The shadow of this old house which has never seen much happiness within it will soon rest upon your thoughtless head. What that will not do, your own inherent weakness will. The woman who trifles with a strong man’s heart has a flaw in her nature which will work out her own destruction in time. I can afford to let you enjoy your prospective honeymoon in peace. Afterwards—” He cast a threatening look towards the decaying structure behind me, and was silent. But that silence did not unloose my tongue. I was absolutely speechless.
> “Ten brides have crossed yonder threshold,” he presently went on in a low musing tone freighted with horrible fatality. “One—and she was the girl whose mother was driven up to these doors dead—lived to take her grandchildren on her knees. The rest died early, and most of them unhappily. Oh, I have studied the traditions of your future home! *You* will live, but of all the brides who have triumphed in the honorable name of Knollys, you will lead the saddest life and meet the gloomiest end notwithstanding you stand before me now, with loose locks flying in the wind, and a heart so gay that even my despair can barely pale the roses on your cheek.”
> This was the raving of a madman. I recognized it as such, and took a little heart. How could he see into my future? How could he prophesy evil to one over whom he will have no control? to one watched over and beloved by a man like Charles? He is a dreamer, a fanatic. His talk about the flaw in my nature is nonsense, and as for the fate lowering over my head, in the shadows falling from the toppling old house in which I am likely to take up my abode—that is only frenzy, and I would be unworthy of happiness to heed it. As I realized this, my indignation grew, and, uttering a few contemptuous words, I was hurrying away when he stopped me with a final warning.
> “Wait!” he said, “women like you cannot keep either their joys or their miseries to themselves. But I advise you not to take Charles Knollys into your confidence. If you do, a duel will follow, and if I have not the legal acumen of your intended, I have an eye and a hand before which he must fall, if our passions come to an issue. So beware! never while you live betray what has passed between us at this interview, unless the weariness of a misplaced affection should come to you, and with it the desire to be rid of your husband.”
> A frightful threat which, unfortunately perhaps, has sealed my lips. Oh, why should such monsters live!

===Chinese===
> 「你把一个男人最珍贵的感情当了儿戏，」他说。「你使我相信，我只消开口，你便归我所有。你究竟是单纯的愚蠢，还是存心的歹毒？你可曾对我有过一丝真心，还是只想着给你裙下的追随者再添一个数？这个人」（说到这里，他的手颤巍巍地指向那座宅子）「享到了那份被拒于我的幸福。他的手碰过你的手，他的嘴唇——」到这里，他的话几乎不成语句，直到那股执念给了他气力，他喊道：「可是纵然如此，纵然你们交换过什么誓约，我对你的权利，绝不放弃。我，在你之前不曾爱过任何女人的我，要在你的命运里插上一只手，教你永远休想把我施加于你的影响从身上剥离。我不会闯到你和你的情人中间；我不会佯装厌恶，也不会拿我的恨、我的痴情虚张声势，搅扰你的外在小日子；可我要在你的隐秘念头上做工夫，在你心宫最深的圣所里，一点一点造起一团越积越重的恐惧，直到你但愿自己当年招惹的是路途上最毒的毒蛇，而不是奥巴代亚·特罗姆这股蛰伏的狂怒。你如今还是个姑娘；等你嫁了人、做了母亲，你就会懂我。眼下我放过你。这座老宅子里从没见过多少幸福，它的阴影很快就要罩上你那没心没肺的脑袋。它办不到的，你与生俱来的弱点自会办到。玩弄一个坚强男人的心的女人，天性里便带了一道裂纹，时候一到，那裂纹自会要了她的命。我大可以容你安安静静享受你在望的蜜月。往后——」他朝我身后那座朽败的建筑投去威胁的一瞥，随即沉默了。可那沉默也没有解放我的舌头。我彻底说不出话来。
> 「十位新娘跨过那边那道门槛，」过了一会儿，他接着往下说，声音低沉悠忽，载满了可怕的宿命。「其中一位——就是她母亲被人用马车载到这几扇门前、载来时已是一具死人的那位姑娘——活到了把孙儿孙女抱上膝头的年纪。其余的都死得早，而且大半死得凄凉。哦，你未来之家的种种掌故，我是下过工夫的！*你*会活下来；可在所有凯旋般嫁入诺利斯这体面姓氏的新娘里，你将过着最凄惨的日子，落得最阴郁的下场——尽管你此刻站在我面前，散发在风里飞扬，一颗心欢快得连我的绝望也几乎冲不淡你颊上的红玫瑰。」
> 这是一个疯子的呓语。我认出它本是呓语，便稍稍壮了些胆。他怎能看透我的未来？他怎能对一个他永远无从左右的人预言灾祸？对一个被查尔斯这样的男人呵护着、深爱着的人预言灾祸？他是个空想家，一个狂徒。他说的我天性里的裂纹，全是胡言；至于悬在我头顶、藏在我多半要定居的那座摇摇欲坠的老宅投下的阴影里的命运——那不过是癫狂，我若把它放在心上，便配不上幸福了。想到这里，我的义愤涨了起来，丢下几句鄙夷的话，正要匆匆走开，他却用最后一句警告拦住了我。
> 「站住！」他说，「你这样的女人，无论欢喜还是苦难，都守不住，藏不下。可我劝你，莫把查尔斯·诺利斯引为心腹。你若引了，一场决斗随之便到；论法律上的精明，我不及你那位未婚夫，可我有一双眼、一只手，一旦你我二人的激情狭路相逢、见了分晓，他必定栽在它们跟前。所以当心！此生此世，永不许泄露你我这场会面里说过的话——除非有朝一日，错托的深情叫你生了倦意，你随之起了摆脱你丈夫的念头。」
> 一个可怕的威胁，而不幸的或许是，它果真封住了我的嘴。哦，这样的怪物，为什么偏要活在世上！

===Original===
> I have been all through the house today with old Mr. Knollys. Every room was opened for my inspection, and I was bidden to choose which should be refurnished for my benefit. It was a gruesome trip, from which I have returned to my own little nook of chintz as to a refuge. Great rooms which for years have been the abode of spiders, are not much to my liking, but I chose out two which at least have fireplaces in them, and these are to be made as cheerful as circumstances will permit. I hope when I again see them, it will not be by the light of a waning November afternoon, when the few leaves still left to flutter from the trees blow, soggy and wet, against the panes of the solitary windows, or lie in sodden masses at the foot of the bare trunks, which cluster so thickly on the lawn as to hide all view of the highroad. I was meant for laughter and joy, flashing lights, and the splendors of ballrooms. Why have I chosen, then, to give up the great world and settle down in this grimmest of grim old houses in a none too lively village? I think it is because I love Charles Knollys, and so, no matter how my heart sinks in the dim shadows that haunt every spot I stray into, I will be merry, will think of Charles instead of myself, and so live down the unhappy prophecies uttered by the wretch who, with his venomous words, has robbed the future of whatever charm my love was likely to cast upon it. The fact that this man left the town today for a lengthy trip abroad should raise my spirits more than it has. If we were going now, Charles and I—But why dream of a Paradise whose doors remain closed to you? It is here our honeymoon is destined to be passed; within these walls and in sight of the bare boughs rattling at this moment against the panes.
> I made a misstatement when I said that I had gone into all the rooms of the house this afternoon. *I did not enter the Flower Parlor.*

===Chinese===
> 今天我跟着诺利斯老先生把整座宅子走了一遍。每一间屋都开了让我察看，还吩咐我挑出哪几间该为我重新添置家具。这是一趟阴森森的行程，走完回到我自己那方印花布小天地，就像回到了一处避难所。多年充作蜘蛛巢穴的大房间，不合我的胃口，可我拣出了两间，里头至少有壁炉，这两间要收拾得环境所许得下的多多亮堂。但愿我再见它们时，不是在十一月下午天光将尽的暮色里——那时候，树上残存的几片叶子，湿漉漉、沉甸甸地扑打在孤零零的窗玻璃上，或者一团团瘫在光秃秃的树干脚下；那些树在草地上密密匝匝，把大路的景致遮了个严实。我生来是为欢笑和快乐、为华灯和舞厅盛景而造的。那我为什么竟舍弃了大千世界，到这个不大有生气的小村子里、这座阴森中的至阴森的老宅里安顿下来？我想，是因为我爱查尔斯·诺利斯；所以，无论我的心怎样在我信步所至的每一处萦绕的幽暗里下沉，我都要快活，都要想着查尔斯而不是我自己，好把那个恶人吐出的不祥预言活活压下去——他用他那些恶毒的话，把我这份爱情本该投在未来上的光彩掠夺得一干二净。这个人今天已离镇远行去了，这本该使我的精神更振作些，可并没有。倘若现在动身的是我们，查尔斯和我——可是，何必去梦一座大门对你紧闭着的天堂呢？命里注定，我们的蜜月要在这里度过；在这几堵墙里，在望得见那此刻正拍打着窗玻璃簌簌作响的光秃枝桠的地方。
> 我先前说今天下午宅子里的房间我都走遍了，这话有失准确。*我没有走进花房。*

===Original===
> I had been married a month and had, as I thought, no further use for this foolish diary. So one evening when Charles was away, I attempted to burn it.
> But when I had flung myself down before the blazing logs of my bedroom fire (I was then young enough to love to crouch for hours on the rug in my lonely room, seeking for all I delighted in and longed for in the glowing embers), some instinct, or was it a premonition? made me withhold from destruction a record which coming events might make worthy of preservation. That was five years ago, and today I have reopened the secret drawer in which this simple book has so long lain undisturbed, and am once more penning lines destined perhaps to pass into oblivion together with the others. Why? I do not know. There is no change in my married life. I have no trouble, no anxiety, no reason for dread; yet—Well, well, some women are made for the simple round of domestic duties, and others are as out of place in the nursery and kitchen as butterflies in a granary. I want just the things Charles cannot give me. I have home, love, children, all that some women most crave, and while I idolize my husband and know of nothing sweeter than my babies, I yet have spells of such wretched weariness, that it would be a relief to me to be a little less comfortable if only I might enjoy a more brilliant existence. But Charles is not rich; sometimes I think he is poor, and however much I may desire change, I cannot have it. Heigho! and, what is worse, I haven’t had a new dress in a year; I who so love dress, and become it so well! Why, if it is my lot to go shabby, and tie up my dancing ringlets with faded ribbons, was I made with the figure of a fairy and given a temperament which, without any effort on my part, makes me, diminutive as I am, the centre of every group I enter? If I were plain, or shy, or even self-contained, I might be happy here, but now—There! there! I will go kiss little William, and lay Loreen’s baby arm about my neck and see if the wicked demons will fly away. Charles is too busy for me to intrude upon him in that horrid Flower Parlor.

===Chinese===
> 我成婚已有一个月，自以为这本愚蠢的日记再无用处了。于是一天晚上，趁查尔斯不在家，我想把它烧掉。
> 可当我扑倒在卧室炉里熊熊燃烧的柴木跟前（那时我还年轻，喜欢在冷清的屋子里蜷在毯子上，一蹲就是几个钟头，在通红的余烬里寻我所喜、觅我所盼），某种本能——或者那竟是预感？——叫我把这本记录从毁灭里留下了，将来之事也许会使它值得保存。那是五年前；今天，我重新打开了那只秘密抽屉——这本朴素的小书在里面安安静静躺了这么久——又一次落笔写下这些行，它们的命运，或许同先前那些一起湮没无闻。为什么？我不知道。我的婚后生活毫无变化。我没有烦恼，没有忧虑，没有害怕的理由；然而——唉，唉，有些女人天生适合操持家务的简朴循环，另一些女人在育儿室和厨房里的格格不入，就像蝴蝶落进了谷仓。我想要的，恰恰是查尔斯给不了我的东西。我有家，有爱，有孩子，有某些女人最渴慕的一切；我崇拜我的丈夫，也知道没有什么比我的娃娃们更甜；可我仍会一阵阵犯那样难堪的倦怠，只要能换一种更光鲜的生活，哪怕过得清苦些，我倒觉得松口气。可查尔斯不富；有时我想他是穷的；无论我多盼望变化，也变不成。哎呀！更糟的是，我整整一年没添过一件新衣裳了——我这么爱穿、又这么穿得好看的一个人！哼，倘若我命该衣衫寒酸，拿褪色的缎带把我欢蹦乱跳的鬈发一束束扎起，那造物又何苦给我仙子般的身段，再给我一种脾性——我不费吹灰之力，凭我这小不点的身量，就能叫我走进的每一群人都围着我转？倘若我生得平常，或者怕羞，或者哪怕自持些，我在这里或许也会快活；可如今——罢了！罢了！我要去亲亲小威廉，把洛琳娃娃般的小胳膊搂上我的脖子，看看那些恶鬼会不会飞走。查尔斯太忙，那可怕的花房，我是不便去搅扰他的。

===Original===
> I was never superstitious till I entered this house; but now I believe in every sort of thing a sane woman should not. Yesterday, after a neglect of five years, I brought out my diary. Today I have to record in it that there was a reason for my doing so. Obadiah Trohm has returned home. I saw him this morning leaning over his fence in the same place and in very much the same attitude as on that day when he frightened me so, a month before my wedding.
> But he did not frighten me today. He merely looked at me very sharply and with a less offensive admiration than in the early days of our first acquaintance. At which I made him my best courtesy. I was not going to remind him of the past in our new relations, and he, thankful perhaps for this, took off his hat with a smile I am trying even yet to explain to myself. Then we began to talk. He had travelled everywhere and I had been nowhere; he wore the dress and displayed the manners of the great world, while I had only a hungry desire to do the same. As for fashion, I needed all my beauty and the fading sparkle of my old animation to enable me to hold up my head before him.
> But as for liking him, I did not. I could admire his appearance, but he himself attracted me no more than when he had words of angry fury on his tongue. He is a gentleman, and one who has seen the world, but in other ways he is no more to be compared with my Charles than his pert new house, built in his absence, with the grand old structure with whose fatality he once threatened me.
> I do not think he wants to threaten me with disaster now. Time closes such wounds as his very effectually. I wish we had some of his money.

===Chinese===
> 走进这座宅子以前，我从不迷信；可如今，一个头脑清醒的女人不该信的种种，我样样都信了。昨天，闲置了五年之后，我又取出了我的日记。今天我必须在里面记下：我这样做，是有缘由的。奥巴代亚·特罗姆回家了。今天早晨我看见他倚在他家栅栏上，还在老地方，那姿势同我婚前一个月他把我狠狠吓着的那一天，几乎一模一样。
> 可今天他没有吓着我。他只是非常锐利地看了我一眼，眼里的倾慕已不像我们初识的头些日子那样咄咄逼人。对此，我还了他一个顶漂亮的屈膝礼。在我们如今的新关系里，我无意向他重提旧事；而他呢，或许正是感念这一点，摘下帽子，露出一丝微笑——那笑意，我到今天还在试着向自己解释。随后我们攀谈起来。他行遍天下，我却哪儿也不曾去过；他穿着大都会的衣装，摆着大世界的做派，而我空有一肚子如饥似渴的向往。至于时髦，我须拿出全部的美貌、加上我旧日神采将褪未褪的光亮，才抬得起头站在他面前。
> 可说到喜欢他，我并不。他的仪表我尽可欣赏，但他这个人对我的吸引，并不比他舌尖挂着狂怒字眼的时候多出一分。他是位绅士，也是见过世面的人；可在别的方面，他没法同我的查尔斯相比，正如他那座他不在家时新盖起的神气新宅，没法同那座庄严的老建筑相比——他当年正是拿那老宅的宿命来恐吓我的。
> 我想他如今不会再拿灾祸来恐吓我了。时间自会把这样的创口弥合得严严实实。但愿我们能有他的一份钱财。

===Original===
> I have always heard that the wives of the Knollys, whatever their misfortune, have always loved their husbands. I do not think I am any exception to the rule. When Charles has leisure to give me an hour from his musty old books, the place here seems lively enough, and the children’s voices do not sound so shrill. But these hours are so infrequent. If it were not for Mr. Trohm’s journal (Did I mention that he had lent me a journal of his travels?) I should often eat my heart out with loneliness. I am beginning to like the man better as I follow him from city to city of the old world. If he had ever mentioned me in its pages, I would not read another line in it, but he seems to have expended both his love and spite when he bade me farewell in the garden underlying these bleak old walls.

> I am becoming as well acquainted with Mr. Trohm’s handwriting as with my own. I read and read and read in his journal, and only stop when the dreaded midnight hour comes with its ghostly suggestions and the unaccountable noises which make this old dwelling so uncanny. Charles often finds me curled up over this book, and when he does he sighs. Why?

===Chinese===
> 我一向听说，诺利斯家的媳妇们无论命运多舛，始终都爱她们的丈夫。我想我也并不例外于这条规矩。查尔斯得闲从他那一堆发霉的旧书里匀给我一个钟头的时候，这地方便显得够热闹了，孩子们的嗓音也不那么尖利了。可这样的钟头太稀罕了。若不是特罗姆先生的游记（我提过他把一本旅行游记借给了我吗？），我常常要孤单得柔肠寸断。跟着他在旧大陆一城一城地走，我开始有点喜欢这个人了。倘若他在书页里提到过我一个字，我便再不会去读它一行；可他的爱与他的怨，似乎都已在那道别里耗尽了——就在这些荒寒老墙底下那座园子里，他向我道的那一声别。

> 特罗姆先生的笔迹，我已熟悉得同我自己的笔迹一样了。我在他的游记里读了又读、读了又读，只有当那叫人心惊的午夜时分带着它鬼影幢幢的联想、带着那些没来由的响动来临时才停下——正是那些响动，叫这座老宅显得如此邪性。查尔斯常撞见我蜷着身子伏在这本书上；每回撞见，他都叹气。这是为什么？

===Original===
> I have been teaching Loreen to dance. Oh, how merry it has made me! I think I will be happier now. We have the large upper hall to take steps in, and when she makes a misstep we laugh, and that is a good sound to hear in this old place. If I could only have a little money to buy her a fresh frock and some ribbons, I would feel perfectly satisfied; but I do believe Charles is getting poorer and poorer every day; the place costs so much to keep up, he says, and when his father died there were debts to be paid which leaves us, his innocent inheritors, very straitened. Master Trohm has no such difficulties. He has money enough. But I don’t like the man for all that, polite as he is to us all. He seems to quite adore Loreen, and as to William, he pets him till I feel almost uncomfortable at times.

> What shall I do? I am invited to New York, *I*, and Charles says I may go, too—only I have nothing to wear. Oh, for some money! a little money! it is my right to have some money; but Charles tells me he can only spare enough to pay my expenses, that my Sunday frock looks very well, and that, even if it did not, I am pretty enough to do without fine clothes, and other nonsense like that—sweet enough, but totally without point, in fact. If I am pretty, all the more I need a little finery to set me off, and, besides, to go to New York without money—why, I should be perfectly miserable. Charles himself ought to realize this, and be willing to sell his old books before he would let me go into this whirl of temptation without a dollar to spend. As he don’t, I must devise some plan of my own for obtaining a little money, for I won’t give up my trip—the first offered me since I was married—and neither will I go away and come back without a gift for my two girls, who have grown to womanhood without a jewel to adorn them or a silk dress to make them look like gentlemen’s children. But how get money without Charles knowing it? Mr. Trohm is such a good friend, he might lend me a little, but I don’t know how to ask him without recalling to his mind certain words long since forgotten by him perhaps, but never to be forgotten by me, featherbrained as many people think me. Is there anyone else?

===Chinese===
> 我一直在教洛琳跳舞。哦，这叫我多么快活！我想往后我会快活些了。我们有宽敞的楼上门厅好踏舞步，她踏错一步，我们就笑，这声音在这老宅子里听着，真叫人心里舒坦。只要能有一点钱，给她添一件新鲜裙衫、几条缎带，我便心满意足了；可我确实觉得查尔斯一天比一天穷；这地方开销太大，他说；他父亲去世时又留下一屁股要还的债，害得我们这些清白无辜的继承人捉襟见肘。特罗姆少爷可没有这样的难处。他的钱有的是。可尽管他对我们全家客客气气，我还是不喜欢这个人。他似乎相当疼爱洛琳；至于威廉，他宠他宠得叫我有时几乎不自在。

> 这可怎么好？有人请我去纽约，*我*！查尔斯说我也可以去——只是我没有可穿的衣服。哦，要是有几个钱就好了！一点点钱！有几个钱本是我的权利；可查尔斯告诉我，他只匀得出够我花销的那一点，说我那件礼拜天穿的裙衫很好看，说就算不好看，凭我这张脸，不穿好衣裳也尽够了，还有诸如此类的胡话——甜是够甜的，可句句不在点子上。我若是漂亮，那才更该有点儿华饰来衬托；再说，身无分文地去纽约——哎呀，那我还不得苦闷死。查尔斯本人该明白这个理儿，宁可卖掉他的旧书，也不该叫我一个子儿不带，就跳进那个花样百出的诱惑漩涡里去。他既不肯这样，我只好自己想个法子弄一点钱：这趟出门我不肯放弃——成婚以来头一遭有人请我——我也断不肯空着手去、空着手回，不给我的两个女儿带份礼——她们都已长成大姑娘了，却从没有一件首饰妆点，没有一件绸衣裳，好叫她们看起来像个体面人家的孩子。可怎么才能弄到钱，又不叫查尔斯知道呢？特罗姆先生是这么好的朋友，兴许肯借我一点；可我不知道怎么向他开口，才不至于叫他想起某些话——那些话他也许早已忘干净了，我却一辈子忘不掉，尽管许多人以为我没心没肺。此外还有谁呢？

===Original===
> I wonder if some things are as wicked as people say they are. I—

===Chinese===
> 不知道有些事情，是不是真像人们说的那样邪恶。我——

===Original===
Here the diary breaks off abruptly. But we know what followed. The forgery, the discovery of it by her suave but secret enemy, his unnatural revenge, and the never-dying enmity which led to the tragic events it has been my unhappy fortune to relate at such length. Poor Althea! with thy name I write finis to these pages. May the dust lie lightly on thy breast under the shadow of the Flower Parlor, through which thy footsteps passed with such dread in the old days of thy youthful beauty and innocence!

===Chinese===
日记到这里戛然而止。可后来的事，我们尽已知晓。那桩伪造，那位温文而阴秘的仇人对它的发现，他那丧尽天良的报复，以及那至死不泯的仇怨——正是这一切，引出了那一连串惨事，我不幸偏偏不得不把它们讲述得这样冗长。可怜的阿尔西娅！我以你的名字，为这些篇页写下终笔。愿尘土轻轻覆在你的胸前，在花房的阴影之下——那间花房，在你青春貌美、纯真无邪的往昔岁月里，你的脚步曾怀着那样的恐惧，从其间一次次穿过！
